757. **Thu 08 Oct 02:05** — **Run 118 review.** **#777 @ cf7bb9db MERGE**, 0 must-fix, 2 LOW (read by me directly, head re-checked after posting). (1) The JSON text is made once before the loop and the swap is one File.Replace with no backup, so the file is never wrong or half written. (2) Bounded at 6 tries, 40+80+120+160+200 = 600 ms; only IO and access-denied errors are retried; the last failure goes to the existing log-and-continue catch. (3) A 0.6 s worst case is acceptable: the normal cost is one 40 ms wait on a rare collision; the wait sits inside SettingsService _lock, which already serialised writes and stops two saves sharing the .tmp. (4) The temp file is used up on success (test 1 checks this); if every try fails it is left behind as before and overwritten next time. (5) Tests +41 -0, no existing assertion changed; the new test checks CD_Settings.json is byte-identical; no Settings key. LOWs: a read-only file now costs 0.6 s on every save; test 1 has about 4x timing margin. Merges clean on rel-20261008c (on its tip). **No PR above #777.** **C-21 heads unchanged:** #762 250baf89 → #763 7a608105, #766 ad231728, #747 384b4e54, #774 c03182ed. **C-21 plain MERGE = 6 with #777** (base rel-20261008c, clean; independent of the others except REGRESSION_LOG, keep both). Proven: the NETLOAD line is built from constants only and names one path; a second word is refused; the deny, setvar, bare-word and lock lists equal the original's (49 of 49 offline tests re-run); SECURELOAD non-zero with the folder not trusted refuses and sends nothing; the 20 s wait cannot be blocked by a modal question (3 s send timeout).
     1. HIGH: the pinned hash sits next to the DLL in a folder Authenticated Users can modify, so a swap of both files loads anything. Pin the SHA256 as a constant in the driver.
     2. MEDIUM (TOCTOU): hold the DLL open with share = Read only, hash from that open handle, keep it open until loaded or timed out. That blocks writes, deletes and folder renames while AutoCAD reads it. No private copy is needed.
     3. MEDIUM: allow exactly the DLL and its `.sha256` in the folder, no subfolders, and no link anywhere on the folder path.
     4. MEDIUM: no title guess matches AutoCAD's known question "File Loading - Security Concern". Use `*Security*`, `*Unsigned*`, `*File Loading*`, Esc only.
     5. LOW: a rejected send must throw BUSY, with no wait, ^C^C or alarm.
     6. LOW: a second load in the same AutoCAD always times out. Refuse "already loaded" by acad process id.
     - **Do not run `witness load` in AutoCAD until fixes 1 to 4 are in.** Promoting the copy into `acad_driver.ps1` stays a separate step, after the pilot and Tim's word.

948. **Driver `esc` (`PATCH_esc`) MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 0 gating, 3 LOW.** Proved by reading the code:
   1. **It can send nothing but Esc.** `SendEscToFrame` has exactly two `keybd_event` calls, both the constant 0x1B (down, up); no key, text or count comes from the caller; `esc` refuses any word after it; `type` still refuses control characters; one key per call, no loop, no retry (a test checks). `ShowWindow` (restore if minimised) and `SetForegroundWindow` move the window, they press nothing.
   2. **Only when a command is active.** CMDACTIVE 0 = "nothing to cancel", no key. CMDACTIVE unreadable while AutoCAD answers = refused. Note: at a real prompt AutoCAD usually refuses COM, so CMDACTIVE cannot be read; then "COM busy" stands in for "CMDACTIVE above 0", the dialog bit cannot be checked, and the guard against a dialog is the main-window-enabled check (a modal dialog disables it). That is sound.
   3. **Only under the lock and only on a test copy.** The lock must be held AND named for the caller, checked before AutoCAD is touched; the drawing guard is the one `type` uses (live path, or the strict 10-minute cache while busy, which also needs one acad.exe, the same pid and the window title naming that drawing). Only a CE prompt: the newest `[CE]` line in the last 40 log lines with no `Command:` after it. Run 5's history shows the real prompt carries it: "[CE] Pipe placed. Pick the next pipe's first point, or press Esc to stop."
   4. **Cannot cancel a prompt in a real drawing, with one narrow exception:** while busy, the title check is a substring match on the file name. A real drawing whose name contains the test copy's name (for example `OLD_WIT5_B1.dwg`), open in the same AutoCAD within 10 minutes, would pass. The pilot's `WIT*_B1` names make this practically impossible. **LOW fix:** in `Invoke-EscSub`, refuse unless the acad.exe pid is the one the driver launched or the lock names (the owner check `quit -Kill` already uses), and match the title on `[<name>]` exactly.
   5. **LOW, wording:** the log and README call it "the only key route in the whole driver". Not quite: `PostEsc` (already in the original, to allow-listed popups) and the SendKeys fallback (allow-list empty) exist. It IS the only route that presses a real keyboard key. Correct the words.
   6. **LOW, focus:** the focus change (AttachThreadInput then SetForegroundWindow) takes the keyboard from whatever is in front, and between the last foreground check and the key there are microseconds in which another window could take it. **Condition, not a code fix:** run `esc` only in a free window with nobody at the PC.

   2. **Still only when a command is active:** CMDACTIVE 0 = "nothing to cancel"; unreadable while AutoCAD answers = refused; bit 8 (dialog) = refused; a disabled main window = refused. The new evidence is a second way to show a CE prompt and gets round none of these.

The pattern `^(CD...|PIT|PITEDIT|...|CE[MS]...|CE_...)$` is anchored at both ends, so LINE, CENTERLINE and PITCH are refused (tests). Any later echo of another command, or a bare `Command:`, ends the evidence. The last line must end with a colon. An echo more than 40 lines back is refused (the safe way). One gap, harmless: a plain command started with no echo (a macro with CMDECHO 0) during or after CD would look like CD. That can only happen on a test copy under the lock, and Esc then cancels that command.

This guard is shared with `type`, so `type` is tightened too. 8 new tests (70 to 78).

A real drawing in the SAME AutoCAD would have to be the active drawing for the key to reach it, and the active-drawing guard (live, or the cache with the whole-name title match) refuses that.

   1. **LOW, before D1d:** a bare Enter at an idle `Command:` prompt REPEATS the last command. If D1c did not leave CD at "Select point 3 (or Enter for straight pipe):" (for example a refused point), D1d would restart CD or repeat something else. **Fix:** send D1d only when the D1c result's last line is that point-3 prompt. Otherwise record it, `esc`, and go to T1.

   2. **Narrow on purpose:** the exception applies only to exactly two constructors, with an empty wrapper whose declaration matches token for token and is preceded by `{`, `}` or `;`, and an exact target. The direct-call order is then checked on the target.
   3. **Every hostile case still fails:** overloads, attributes, `#if`, a third constructor, work inside the wrapper, a wrong scale function, a wrong parameter type, and two wrappers or two targets are all rejected. A later wrapper change fails safe.

## OPUS REVIEW 17 Oct (desk plan/03, Max agent): Sonnet builder PRs 1259-1286 (C-39 and S-37)

995. **Sat 17 Oct 00:10** — OPUS REVIEWER (Opus 5.5, claude-opus-5-5, Max agent, read only on the PRs: no code edited, nothing merged, no AutoCAD, nothing touched the live bundle). Scope: the builder PRs marked "needs Opus review". Civil/Manager on C-39 (`claude/rel-20261010e`, tip d36f9545): #1259 (fix head 4b1477b3), #1263, #1266, #1268, #1269, #1271, #1274, #1275, #1276, #1277, #1279, #1280, #1281, #1283, #1284, plus #1286 from TEAMLANE4 (the BUG-586 repair). Structural on S-37 (`claude/rel-20261010d`, tip 0dfbd1d6): #1264, #1265, #1267, #1270, #1272, #1273. Every head was fetched and read; every PR is 0 commits behind its base. No PR comments posted (log only, as briefed). Totals: **20 MERGE, 2 MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 0 HOLD, 0 REJECT** (22 PRs), plus two closures recommended (#1256, #1258: superseded, entry 1019). **Built here:** the whole Civil chain below, merged in order onto C-39 in a scratch worktree, compiles for net10 with its tests (Drainage + Drainage.Tests, 0 errors). Not built here: the Civil net48 lane and the Structural lanes (see entry 1019).
996. **The three things that matter, the answer first.**
   1. **#1276 can write a WRONG invert where the drawing used to say Unknown.** A real LandXML pit (Civil 3D export) has one `<Invert>` per connected pipe, each naming its pipe (`refPipe`) and whether it flows in or out (`flowDir`). The parser keeps only the level and puts every one at the pit centre, so the shared mapper's "nearest text" choice is a tie for every pipe end and the result is whichever pair the sort happens to put first. Codex's test case has one pipe per pit, so it passes. Fix in entry 1004.
   2. **#1265 fixes a cause that has only been read from the code.** CEHATCHAUDIT and CEHATCHCHANGE now read each hatch the way new hatches already were (each risky reading in its own short transaction). That is the right pattern and nothing else changes, but the eInvalidKey stop was never reproduced; only a Codex or Tim run in AutoCAD 2027 proves it.
   3. **Merge order traps.** #1274 and #1275 each contain all six #1259 commits (they merged the Codex branch), so #1259 must go in first. #1271 is based on #1266's branch. #1286 and #1281 both add a Settings group in two places of `UserSettings.cs`; keep both blocks, and check the shared `/// <summary>` line and the class's closing brace survive (a quick keep-both in my trial lost both and broke the build until fixed by hand).
997. **#1259 @ 4b1477b3 MERGE** (fix round for entries 981 and 988). The council export now prints exactly what the Catchments window shows: `CaptureCatchments` reads `row.FlowDisplay` and `source.TotalFlowDisplay`, and the total carries no "L/s" after Unknown. Checked that nothing else in the export reads `TotalQ`. LOW (b) closed (the window total hides " L/s" while Unknown), LOW (c) closed (comments mark that the area is set before its source), LOW (a) closed (BUG-593 and BUG-596 entries put back in order, stray line gone). Six new cases, five RED on the old head as the log says (read, not rerun). Still based on `claude/rel-20261010b`; onto C-39 it clashes only at the REGRESSION_LOG top.
998. **#1263 @ da006db6 MERGE, 1 LOW** (BUG-650: Cancel in CDFW or CDSOURCE blanked pit inverts). Two parts, both right. (1) The record refresh now sets a pit's invert from its plan label only when the label has an "IL" line; a label showing only "CL 21.000" leaves the stored 20.000 alone, and the cover level still follows the label. The parser sets `ShowInvert` only when it finds an IL line, so the guard is real. (2) CDFW and CDSOURCE no longer run the full drawing refresh (which saved straight away) before the first pick; they bind the drawing's stored record without writing, the same pattern CD already uses, and `SourcePlacementService.Place` still syncs after Accept. LOW: a label with no IL line still switches that pit's "show invert" off in the record (unchanged behaviour, level kept); say so in the AutoCAD checklist so a missing IL on the next relabel is not read as this bug.
999. **#1266 @ 9c2e3d50 MERGE, 1 LOW** (BUG-589: first CDCHECK after reopening missed the wrong FALL arrow). The fit and FALL arrow checks now read `TryReadProjectWithoutChanging`: the live record when it is this drawing's, else the saved record through an open-close read; nothing is loaded, synced or saved. A drawing with no saved record gives an empty record, not the "could not be read" line, so plain drawings do not get a false warning (checked). LOW: every guard is source text; no test reproduces "reopen, then CDCHECK". The Codex retest is the proof.
1000. **#1271 @ 48f3480a MERGE after #1266** (BUG-588: the saved CDCHECK list lacked the FALL arrow findings). One helper builds the extra lines in command-line order, FALL arrows uncapped (every finding, not the console's 60), then open ends, then the "could not be read" sentence. Same review result the command line prints, no second calculation. Retarget to C-39 once #1266 is in.
1001. **#1268 @ 3fb0ddbb MERGE, 2 LOW** (BUG-649: an AG drain rising 200 mm to its outlet was told it meets 1:100). The outlet end is the run end nearest the picked outlet (pit insertion point, or nearest point on a pipe), read open-close; a tie within 1 unit, no outlet, or an unreadable outlet stays Unknown and reads exactly as today. A run that rises towards its outlet gets "falls the wrong way, rises 200 mm towards outlet P2" and never "meets". Warning only, the drain is still placed (R-27). LOW: the placed message appends "." after the grade text, so the new sentence ends "outlet.." (the old "shorten the run.." had the same fault). LOW: the 1-unit tie assumes a millimetre drawing, like the rest of CDAGI.
1002. **#1269 @ 137834ed MERGE** (BUG-643: a 4 x 4 m outline with an R2 m semicircle read 16 m2, not 22.28). Each curved edge becomes points at most 1 degree apart, so the existing area and "which pit takes which part" code is unchanged. Checked the bulge maths (centre offset (1 - b^2)/(4b) x chord, left of the chord for a positive bulge), that the arc is densified before the Xref transform (so a mirrored Xref lands right) and that a polyline with its extrusion pointing down flips the bulge. Both CDCATCHMENT and CDPAVE read through the one reader, and no other area path reads polyline corners.
1003. **#1274 @ 7553cea9 MERGE after #1259** (BUG-592: Pipe Flows showed 0.00 L/s for a missing input). Own commit only reviewed (the rest is #1259). A blank, zero, negative or overflowing input gives Unknown in both windows, the saved draft (empty, not 0) and the export (no unit after Unknown). An old draft that saved 0 for an impossible result no longer counts as matching. Carries #1258's `PipeResultAvailabilityRegressionTests` unchanged.
1004. **#1276 @ f6389f40 MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 1 MEDIUM, 2 LOW** (BUG-605/604/602: LandXML import lost the supplied inverts). Right: the duplicate `<Invert>` reading is gone (one level, one entry); the LandXML lane now runs the same shared pipe-end mapper as the X-Civil lane and then fills blank pit levels from the lowest connected pipe end (never overwrites); the result page says "from the LandXML file"; an empty file now says so in the window. **MEDIUM (entry 996.1):** read `refPipe` (and `flowDir`) on each `<Invert>` and give that level to the named pipe's end at this pit; when a pit has more than one `<Invert>` and they cannot be told apart, leave those pipe ends Unknown rather than let the tie decide. Add one test: a pit with an in pipe and an out pipe, two levels, each landing on the right pipe whatever the file order. LOW: the new summary was stacked on top of `DeriveMissingPitInvertsFromPipes`' old one (two summary blocks on one member, the old method lost its own). LOW: the window's "No pits or pipes were read" text is shown for any "import aborted" line; today the LandXML lane has only that one abort, so it is right for now.
1005. **#1275 @ cfd179ea MERGE after #1259, 2 LOW** (BUG-597: Charged System kept old figures beside a warning). Each of the five results is now a figure or Unknown on its own: a blank head leaves the design flow and the losses standing, a blank area takes all five; a typed 0 is a real value for pipe length, loss coefficient and head, missing for the rest; blank or text now parses as missing, not 0. Draft saves empty, never 0. The charged results are not in the export, so no export change was needed (checked). LOW: #1258's `ChargedResultAvailabilityRegressionTests` arrived inside the merge commit 27ecb5c3, not in a normal commit; harmless once merged, but hard to audit. LOW (follow-up, not this PR): the 0.82 inlet discharge coefficient is still fixed in code (R-00).
1006. **#1277 @ be7827b8 MERGE** (BUG-594: a newer Blue Factor report was skipped for an older STORM report). The reader now calls the existing `ParseAnyRatingReport` dispatcher, which already marks a Blue Factor report as a rating report. One line; the test builds real PDFs in %TEMP% (net10 only). Carries #1258's reader guard unchanged.
1007. **#1279 @ c7dd9cda MERGE, 1 LOW** (BUG-607: comparison said OVERALL 100% with pits missing). With anything missing or extra the last line is now "MATCHED-FIELD SCORE ... NOT an overall score: 2 pits missing, 1 pipe missing". Identical records still get "OVERALL SCORE". Nothing reads that line back (checked). LOW: entry 987's wider guard (`(?i)overall.*\b100(\.0+)?\s*%`) was not adopted; the anchored guard is enough for today's wording.
1008. **#1280 @ 2b159615 MERGE, 2 LOW** (BUG-606: CDCHECKDWG wrote its report into the checked DWG's folder). New switch `checks.consultantCheckReportBesideDwg`, OFF: the report goes to the CE report folder and the command line names it; ON is today's behaviour; %TEMP% stays the last fall-back. LOW: confirm the switch shows on the Settings page (R-00), not only in the settings file.
1009. **#1281 @ 4b741083 MERGE, 2 LOW** (BUG-590/591: issue state on page 1 only; Excel numbers saved as text). Every printed page now carries "job | issue state | Page n of m" in the bottom margin (black bold for a draft, grey once issued, no blue); every Excel tab carries an "Issue State" row; plain numbers go to Excel as numbers at the PDF's decimals, while "Unknown", names, "007" and "1 in 100" stay text. Two switches in a new group, both ON (Tim's recommended answers to Q6 and Q7, built as DRAFT). PDF is made through the Print window, which now prints through the stamp. #1258's export guard was changed only to read the pages through the same stamping paginator the Print button uses (legitimate). LOW: no guard proves the Print button calls `CalculationPageStamp.For` (one source check would do). LOW: `FormattedText` uses the obsolete constructor without pixels-per-dip (a warning on both lanes, not an error).
1010. **#1283 @ caa1db83 MERGE** (BUG-573: 20.800 against 20.80 read as a schedule change). Values that are both plain decimals compare by value (no rounding: 20.80 against 20.804 is still a change, "P1" against "p1" too); the stale-override check uses the same rule.

Preview now names the missing linetype with "Load it (LINETYPE, then Load) and preview again", using the same test as Apply's check (layer missing and linetype missing). LOW (Tim's call, follow-up): Apply's refusal is an existing hard stop; under R-27 the profile could load DASHDOT2 from the standard linetype file and warn instead.
1012. **#1286 @ b27c49d2 MERGE after #1281, 2 LOW** (BUG-586/637 Civil half: a custom note beside "With Step Irons" vanished). One cleaner for the Pit Schedule window and the schedule composer: only the phrase in the capitals CE writes, standing alone, is removed; "Inspect cover with step irons before issue" survives; switch `pitScheduleRemarks.keepCustomNoteBesideStepIrons` ON, OFF gives today's whole-line removal. This answers entry 985's MEDIUM for lower-case notes. Carries #1256's guard file byte for byte. LOW: the composer still writes "With Step Irons" as three separate literals; use the new `GeneratedPhrase` so the cleaner and the writer cannot drift. LOW: a drafter's own note typed in title case ("... With Step Irons") is still trimmed; add the lower-case-ending case entry 985 asked for, so the rule is pinned.
1013. **#1264 @ 30e0270a MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 2 LOW** (BUG-583/638/636/637: ABS, DSET, DSET1, DUP on locked layers). Every write is now behind a locked-layer check made on a read-only open: ABS checks the brace's own layer and the layer it would move to (both A and B paths really move it, checked); DSET and DSET1 open for read and upgrade only after the check; DUP now opens only dimensions and leaders for write, so pit blocks and schedule text are no longer opened (which woke Civil's schedule link). CE never unlocks. **Fix (wording the drafter reads):** DUP passes "dimension or leader" to a helper that adds "s", giving "DUP left 2 dimension or leaders alone"; and with no layer name and a count above 1 it says "its layer is locked". Pass the plural in, or say "objects".

Every existing-hatch reading (audit, snapshot, restore check, final checks) goes through one helper that uses the new-hatch path, `CaptureDirectNewPersistedFacts`, whose two tolerant readings each own a short transaction. No transaction of the command is open around them any more (checked every caller; the draw-order read now comes after the hatch readings). The existing contract tests still hold. LOW: the cause is read from the code, not reproduced (entry 996.2), and the audit now opens three short transactions per hatch: not timed on a big drawing.

1017. **#1272 @ 8a1aec7c MERGE, 1 LOW** (BUG-504: CSWINDCLASS saved a blank class; Esc at "Use N2?" counted as No). A blank saves nothing and says "No wind class was given; nothing was saved."; a class outside N1 to N6 and C1 to C4 is saved with a warning (R-27); Esc at "Use N2?" stops the command. LOW: the new Yes/No-or-cancel question is a near copy of the old one (design rule 1); the other questions using the old one still take Esc as No.
1018. **#1273 @ c64fa0c0 MERGE, 1 LOW** (BUG-575: Settings showed 1.0.1.0 while 1.0.116 was running). The box and the status-bar tooltip now always show the loaded version, with an older install record shown beside it in brackets. The box is read-only and never saved back (checked), so the install record is not overwritten. LOW: "install record" is a software word; "(the setup record says 1.0.1.0)" or similar reads plainer.

## OPUS REVIEW 17 Oct, round 2 (desk plan/03, Max agent): Sonnet builder PRs 1278-1295 and the three fix re-checks

1022. **Sat 17 Oct 00:59** — OPUS REVIEWER round 2 (Opus 5.5, claude-opus-5-5, Max agent, read only on the PRs: no code edited, nothing merged, no AutoCAD, nothing touched the live bundle, no PR comments). Same checks as entries 995-1021. Structural on S-37 (`claude/rel-20261010d`, tip 0dfbd1d6): #1278 cfaa62ca, #1282 1251b22f, #1285 6e77bf21, #1287 e877b453, #1294 d153b3fc, #1295 0629b5fd (TEAMLANE2 lists no Structural PR after #1295). Civil on C-39 (`claude/rel-20261010e`, tip d36f9545): #1288 fb04a578, #1289 d49e7026 (TEAMLANE4), #1290 365c287e, #1291 c9509bda, #1292 ea85e9ee, #1293 287f788f. Re-checks: #1276 8a54cef0, #1264 bc0065c8, #1273 f67d2ffc, plus the unannounced fix pushes #1268 72e2295f, #1280 4f939b6e and #1281 558d7666. Every head is 0 commits behind its base. Totals: **12 new PRs: 9 MERGE, 3 MERGE-WITH-FIXES (#1278, #1282, #1285), 0 HOLD, 0 REJECT; the three re-checked fixes (#1276, #1264, #1273) all now MERGE**.
1023. **The three things that matter, the answer first.**
   1. **#1285 puts layout text and break lines in at model-space size.** Moving 25C text and BL into the layout you clicked is right, but both still size themselves from DIMSCALE. On an office drawing (DIMSCALE 100) a note typed on a layout comes out 250 mm tall on the sheet and the BL zigzag 200 mm (150 with #1278). Codex's recipe used DIMSCALE 1, so the test passes and a real job does not. Fix in entry 1026.
   2. **#1278 makes CEHATCH and CEHATCHAUDIT disagree at 1:50.** CEHATCH now places Earth at pattern scale 20 at 1:50, but CEHATCHAUDIT (and its repair) still expects the 1:20 value (8) at every scale except 1:100, so a hatch CEHATCH has just drawn is reported as wrong, and the audit repair would put it back to the 1:20 density. The new straight-line rule itself is safe: every shipped preset is either proportional (8/40, 1/5) or constant (1/1, 20/20, 50/50), so the line through the two stated values IS the proportional rule; it only "invents" a number for a custom preset. Fix in entry 1024.
   3. **#1282 may hand back the right style NAME with the wrong style VALUES.** The restore now makes the saved style current through the drawing (that part is the real BUG-621 cause: the old restore wrote the DIMSTYLE variable, which AutoCAD does not let a program set). It does not copy the style's values back (no `SetDimstyleData`, and not the `-DIMSTYLE Restore` the setup itself uses because "the command path is more reliable"), so Standard may come back carrying TIM LEADER's ByLayer colours and other settings as overrides. Not proved either way; Codex must read the next native dimension's colour and text height, not only the style name. Entry 1025.
1024. **#1278 @ cfaa62ca MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 1 MEDIUM, 3 LOW** (BUG-614/628: hatches and BL at any scale; switch `Layouts.FollowAnyDrawingScale`, ON). Right: one rule for the preview and the placement; 1:20 and 1:100 return exactly today's values; a mixed pair (50/1000) is still refused with a clearer line; BL is 1.5, 2 and 2 mm on paper at every scale (30/40/40 at 1:20, 150/200/200 at 1:100, unchanged); switch OFF gives today's behaviour. CEHATCHCHANGE uses the same `ResolveScale`, so it follows too. **MEDIUM (entry 1023.2):** `HatchUpgradeCommands.ResolveAuditScale` (CEHATCHAUDIT, its repair list and the closest-preset match) still uses the old two-scale rule; route it through `DrawingScaleRule.HatchPatternScale` with today's fall-back when that returns null, and add one test: a hatch placed at 50/500 audits as compliant. LOW: BL takes the annotation scale when DIMSCALE is 0 or 1, the hatches do not (they need the office pair), so "one rule" is two; and a model-space drawing left at DIMSCALE 1 and CANNOSCALE 1:1 now gets a 2-unit zigzag where it used to get 40. LOW: the dimension commands (AD, LD, Q) still refuse every DIMSCALE except 20 and 100 (`ValidateDimScale`), so a 1:50 drawing hatches but cannot dimension; a follow-up, not this PR. LOW: the Settings window row is not built (R-00, Codex's job), and the 1.5/2/2 mm sizes are constants in code.

LOW: `CurrentAppearanceRule.Changed` is a copy of `CommandExecutionService.SystemVariableChanged` (design rule 1). LOW: Settings window row not built (R-00).

LOW: stopping the whole run for one locked Xref is the bug's stated expectation, so R-27 is met by the plain line; moving the unlocked ones and listing the rest would be Tim's call.

1042. **The three things that matter, the answer first.**
   1. **#1308 plus #1276 shows a false message.** Each is right on its own, and git merges them with no conflict. Together, closing the LandXML review window pops a second window saying "No pits or pipes were read from that file, so nothing was drawn", when the file was read fine and the drafter simply said No. Fix in entry 1043.
   2. **#1299, once switched ON, moves windows it should leave alone.** Every CE window not moved before is re-centred on AutoCAD, including windows that place themselves on purpose, and Structural's XCLM text window (which already remembers its own spot) then has two rules fighting. OFF by default, so merging is safe; fix before anyone turns it on. Entry 1046.
   3. **#1310 shows the wrong command on the profile window.** CDPROFILE opens the Calculations window class with the title "Profiles", so the title bar reads "Creative Engineering | Profiles (CDCALC)". A drafter who types CDCALC gets the calculations, not the profile. Entry 1045. (Also #1301 keeps 0.5 s although Tim ruled 0.3 s; entry 1047.)
1043. **#1308 @ 2c490bca MERGE-WITH-FIXES, after #1276, 1 MEDIUM, 2 LOW** (BUG-603 HIGH: LandXML "Read source" wrote the network before anything was accepted). Right, traced in the service, not only in the source guard: the review sits after the in-memory read, flatten, snap and move, and before the first drawing touch (the locked-layer read, the clearing of the earlier import, the first pit insert). The window path reuses the review page X-Civil already uses ("Import to drawing" only after the reading is shown); Close or Esc returns "not continued", so nothing is written and the report says "Import cancelled at the review. Nothing was changed in the drawing." The auto-found-XML path prints the same sentence and asks "Import these into the drawing? [Yes/No] <No>"; a question that fails counts as No. **R-27 (asked):** this is a question, not a block. Yes (or the window button) writes exactly as before, the switch `xcivilImport.landXmlAskBeforeWrite` OFF restores read-and-draw in one step, and BUG-603's own expected result asks for this stop. Default No on Enter is right here because the prompt just before it ("Use 'x.xml'? <Yes>") defaults Yes, so two Enters must not write. **MEDIUM (entry 1042.1):** after #1276 the LandXML result block reads `aborted = an "import aborted" line || IsCancelled(...)` and #1276 added `else ShowReadFailure(LandXmlNothingReadMessage)`, so a cancelled review lands in the read-failure window. Fix in #1308 once #1276 is in its base: test `LandXmlImportReview.IsCancelled(reportLines)` first and show no window at all (the command line already says nothing was changed), keep the read-failure window for the real abort only; and pass `fromLandXml: true` to `BuildImportReviewRows` in the review step (#1276's parameter), so the review page says "from the LandXML file", not "from the pit schedule". Add one test that a cancelled report never reaches the read-failure text. LOW: the move sentence says "mm"; it is drawing units (right on a millimetre drawing, as the rest of the import assumes). LOW: the test file's summary calls the switch `imports.landXmlAskBeforeWrite`; the real key is `xcivilImport.landXmlAskBeforeWrite`. **Follow-up for the carpark (not this PR; found while checking the same gate on the X-Civil side):** CDIMPORTXCIVIL's command-line pre-flight question "Proceed with import? [Yes/No] <Yes>" treats Esc as Yes (`userDeclined` is true only for an OK "No"), so Esc carries on and writes.

The one switch is Tim's existing `rightClick.showCommandNames` (ON, INT-075); Civil sets it before it registers its ribbon (checked in `PluginInitialization.cs`). Civil's ribbon commands are all plain names (checked), so no odd bracket text. **CE_SHARED:** `CeRibbonLabel.cs` (new) and `RibbonBuilder.cs` change, so Manager must ship in the same train with the same CE_SHARED (its release already takes the approved Civil CE_SHARED by hash; just never ship Civil alone). 1060. **The three things that matter, the answer first.**
   1. **#1304: typing F ends MLL/MLR and throws the note away.** The jig takes F, but AutoCAD's drag then returns "keyword" to the caller, and the caller treats anything but OK as Cancel: the note is disposed and the command ends silently. Before this PR F said "Invalid point" and kept the note; after it the typed words are lost. The repo's own jig with keywords (CDRG, `SourceBlockInsertCommands`) loops on the drag for exactly this reason. Fix in entry 1063.
   2. **#1300 fixes a cause nobody has proved.** It rebuilds the dimension picture (`RecomputeDimensionBlock`) after DSET, DSET1 and DUP set the factor, and reads the drawn number back to warn if it still disagrees. That is the right standard step, and the read-back is honest, but whether it cures the "still 5000 after REGEN and reopen" record is only provable in AutoCAD.
   3. **Two code clashes need a hand resolve:** #1300 onto #1264 in `DimensionCommands.cs` (four keep-both hunks), and **#1285 onto #1278 in `BreaklineCommands.cs` (new since round 2: a plain keep-both does NOT compile).** Exact resolutions in entry 1068.
1061. **#1298 @ 80943121 MERGE, 2 LOW** (BUG-632: the section mark letter sits in the clear half of the bubble; switch `Annotation.SectionMarkIdentifierInClearHalf`, ON). Traced the geometry: the half-bubble polyline's bulge puts the solid half opposite the view direction (view Left gives a solid right half, which is BUG-632's record), and the letter now goes to centre + view x (gap + half its extent), so it is in the empty half for all four directions; a long identifier is shrunk until its far corner is inside 0.95 of the radius, and the command line says so once. Switch OFF is the old centre. Bubble, solid half, cut line and sheet text unchanged. Pure rule, 16 cases, scale-invariant. LOW: with the 0.7 character-width estimate a TWO-character identifier at Left or Right (the prompt's own example "01", or "A1") does not fit, so it comes out about 2.3 mm instead of 2.5 and prints "is long, so its letters were drawn smaller"; Up/Down keeps 2.5. Mixed heights on one sheet; measure the real text (its extents after alignment) or shrink only from three characters. LOW: BUG-632 asks for Tim's intended bubble layout before geometry changes; this is the recommended default (DRAFT), and the Settings window row is not built (R-00, Codex).
1062. **#1300 @ 79c6af98 MERGE, 3 LOW** (BUG-635: DSET/DSET1/DUP redraw the dimension and check its number; switch `Options.RebuildDimensionAfterScaleChange`, ON). The rebuild is called only where the factor was actually set, on the dimension already open for write, inside each command's own transaction; nothing is added on an automatic, idle or Undo/Redo path (guardrail 8 item 17: no new transaction, view change, system variable or command between U and REDO; the redo-safe scan passes on the merged train). Read-back: aligned and rotated dimensions only, decimal units only, no rounding step, measured text only ("<>"); everything else is "cannot tell" and silent. Warn only, nothing undone (R-27). LOW: the DSET and DSET1 lines print "the dimension at handle 110B ..."; the handle is a software word on the command line (the BUG-360 rule); keep it in the log, print "reads 5000 but should read about 1000". LOW: "Look at its Linear scale in Properties" should name the real field, "Dim scale linear (Properties, Ctrl+1, Primary Units)". LOW: a prefix with a digit in it ("2x<>" or "2 OFF <>") makes the first number 2, so a correct dimension is reported as wrong; and a style on Windows-desktop units (DIMLUNIT 6) is never judged. The guard is a source check only; the read-back runs inside the same transaction as the rebuild, so if AutoCAD finishes the picture only at commit the warning could appear for a dimension that is right after commit. The Codex retest is the proof.
1063. **#1304 @ d5d64fd3 MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 1 HIGH, 2 LOW** (BUG-622 MLL/MLR F swaps the side; BUG-624 wrong-side note reported). Right: the prompt shows "or [Flip]", F flips the side inside the jig, the final layout uses `jig.Side`, the click is mirrored onto the final side before the side check, and a wrong-side landing (BUG-624's text X right of the arrow X for a left note) prints a plain line and logs the numbers (warn only; cause not proved, said so). The command registry prompts name F. **HIGH (entry 1060.1):** `CreateFixedSideCalloutWithPreview` calls `ed.Drag(jig)` once and disposes the note on any status other than OK. Fix: `do { dragResult = ed.Drag(jig); } while (dragResult.Status == PromptStatus.Keyword);` before the OK check, so F redraws on the other side and the prompt comes back; Escape after F still ends with nothing added (BUG-622 item 7). Add a guard that the drag is repeated on Keyword (a source check is enough) and put "MLL, arrow, two-line note, F, F again, click" in the Codex checklist. LOW: after F the preview does not move until the mouse moves, because the next sample returns NoChange for the same point; set a "side changed" flag so the first sample after F returns OK. LOW: the side check uses one text height as its tolerance in drawing units; fine at 1:100, untested at other scales.
1064. **#1306 @ 30fc0929 MERGE after #1298, 2 LOW** (UX-SEC-01 slice 1: section marks record number and sheet; SM offers the next number; switches `Annotation.SectionMarkOfferNextIdentifier` OFF, `Annotation.SectionMarkSkipLetters` blank). Own commits only reviewed. Every new mark writes "SECMARK, identifier, sheet" as CE data on its cut line (best effort, never stops the command). Checked nothing else reads the `CE_STRUC_SUITE` CE data as anything but a schema tag (SBEAM uses its own application name), so a tagged line is not mistaken for a beam. With the switch ON, SM asks the sheet first and offers the next number on that sheet (3 after 1, 2; 03 after 01, 02; C after A, B, skipping listed letters); mixed or unknown patterns give no offer, never a guess; Enter takes the offer, typing overrides. OFF: the questions and their order are exactly as before. The drawing read is a short read-only transaction inside the command, aborted. LOW: a blank sheet reference means "this sheet", but blank marks on every layout are pooled, so Layout S02 can be offered 3 because S01 has 1 and 2; count blank-sheet marks per layout (the cut line's owner). LOW: the record is written even with the switch OFF (stated in the PR; harmless, but the AutoCAD checklist should say the cut line now carries CE data).

1088. **The three things that matter, the answer first.**
   1. **#1317 (level cascade) does not work in Edit Pipe, the main way it is used.** Edit Pipe writes the new level onto the pipe BEFORE it asks "Recalculate downstream?", so the cascade reads the pit drop against the NEW level. Raise P1's arriving level from 10.00 to 10.20 (outlet 9.95): the "drop" is read as 250 mm and P2 to P3 stays at 9.95, so nothing downstream moves (today it goes to 10.20). Lower it to 9.80: the drop reads as minus 150 mm and the rulebook drop is used instead of the 50 mm the pit had. The builder's tests build the graph with the OLD level, which is the CDPROPAGATE case only. The switch is ON, so this would reach Tim. Fix in entry 1089.
   2. **#1331 hides downpipe junctions in metre drawings.** It now stores the downpipe's chainage in real millimetres, but the junction display (Pit Schedule, long section, Inspector) compares it with the pipe length in drawing units and drops any junction "past the end". In a metre drawing 12,500 against 30 means the junction wording vanishes. The Hunt said only the reported figure was wrong. Fix: convert for the words only. Entry 1103.
   3. **#1318 reads the pit's turn from the record, not the block.** The CD fit check uses the pit record's rotation, which is only written when CD places the pit or Edit Pit saves it. A pit turned with AutoCAD's ROTATE keeps its old record value, so the Hunt case (ROTATE a 600 x 900 pit 90 degrees) still passes a 750 pipe. Read the block's rotation (the pit id is already in hand). Entry 1090.
1089. **#1317 @ 3373b6cd MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 1 HIGH, 1 MEDIUM, 3 LOW** (BUG-652..655, the cascade; switch `linking.cascadeKeepsPitDrop` ON). **(a) Traced:** the pure walk is right for CDPROPAGATE (the drawing still holds the old levels there; a head pit takes no drop; a blank end is skipped, named and stops the walk; the junction takes the lowest untouched arriving pipe and says which). **HIGH (entry 1088.1):** `PipeEditCommand.Update.cs` calls `StorePipeInverts` (line about 1880) before `PromptDownstreamCascadeIfInvertChanged`; `BuildNetworkGraph` reads the pipe's stored levels, so the edited pipe arrives at its NEW level. `oldArrivingLowest` then includes that new level (the `anchorArrivingPipeHandle` exemption only covers `lowestOther`), so `existingDrop = new arriving - outlet`. Worked: raise 10.00 to 10.20 over outlet 9.95 gives drop 0.25 and outlet 9.95, unchanged; lower to 9.80 gives drop -0.15, so the R-08 value is used, not 50 mm. The PR body's "handled both ways" is not right. Fix: pass the edited pipe's OLD arriving level (`oldToInvertSnapshot` is already there) into the plan and use it for that pipe when working out the drop at the anchor pit; add a test whose graph holds the NEW level on the edited pipe (raise and lower), which fails today. **MEDIUM:** `RecomputeWithOverrides` (the preview's grade edits) rebuilds the plan without the anchor pipe (and, after the fix, without its old level); keep both on the plan. **(b) Rules:** R-08 drop comes from the rulebook (Settings-first); the BUG-654 refusal follows the existing flat-grade refusal and offers a way through, so it is not a new hard block, but in Edit Pipe there is no overwrite choice, so "run it again and choose to overwrite" cannot be done there (LOW 1: word it for each route). No hook, no redo-sensitive call; net48 and net10 compile (chain, entry 1111). **(c) I2:** no new window. **(d) ON is right once fixed** (the old levels are the bug). **LOW 2:** the skipped-pipe line shows the pipe's handle in brackets; plain words only. **LOW 3:** no Settings window row (R-00, standing).
1090. **#1318 @ 1a091ee5 MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 1 MEDIUM, 1 LOW** (BUG-675, 678). **(a) Traced:** the label and cover-text step turns the offset into the pit's own frame and back (right, and rotation 0 is the old rule exactly); Edit Pit uses `PlanAnchor.RotationRadians` and the batch uses `PitBatchItem.RotationRadians`, both read from the BLOCK, so BUG-678 is cured on those routes. **MEDIUM (entry 1088.3):** `CdCommand.PitFit` uses `pit.RotationRadians` from the RECORD (`TryResolvePitAtPoint` returns the recorded pit); nothing updates that value when the pit is turned with AutoCAD's ROTATE (only CD placement and Edit Pit save write it). Read the block rotation from `pitId` (one open-close read) and fall back to the record. Add the ROTATE case to the AutoCAD steps. **(b)** No hook, no window, no switch (old behaviour is the bug, unrotated pits unchanged). The upsize side follows the pit's own X/Y, which matches its dynamic properties. **LOW:** a pit turned and resized in the same Edit Pit Save steps its text by the turn it had when the window opened (the PR says so).
1091. **#1319 @ 6e30fc50 MERGE, 0 findings** (BUG-687). **(a) Traced:** `HasDialogChanges` now compares the Q box with the flow the record held when the window opened (prefilled with "0.##", compared within 0.005, blank, unreadable and zero all mean "no flow", the same rule the Save writes with), so a Q-only change reaches the existing save at `Update.cs:1865`; no other writer fills the Q box on opening, so an untouched window still says "No changes detected". **(b-d)** No hook, no window, no switch needed (data-loss bug).
1092. **#1320 @ 2c21b843 MERGE, 1 LOW** (BUG-686). **(a) Traced:** CD and the same-plan break now tell the arrow which levels are real, so a given 0.00 points the arrow from the 0.5 end; a blank end still uses draw order; every other builder of the arrow inputs leaves the new flags off (old rule). These are the only two callers that set `UseInvertsForArrowFlow` from levels. **LOW:** the triage asked for one shared "level is known" helper with BUG-653; this uses flags instead. Fine, but say so in the card.
1093. **#1321 @ c95e3264 MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 1 MEDIUM, 1 LOW** (BUG-684). **(a) Traced:** a curved polyline keeps the source and says so in plain words (cured). **MEDIUM:** a CLOSED polyline now gets its first point added at the end and the source erased, so the pipe starts and ends at the same spot: both ends find the same pit, BUG-060's same-pit rule clears one end, and the drafter gets a loop pipe with one open end; a closed polyline whose last vertex already sits on its first gets a zero-length last side. The Hunt fix was "refused or warned, and the source kept". Do the same as the curved case for a closed line (warn, keep the line, use the points as drawn), or ask. **LOW:** the curved-line message says the line "has been kept" before the pipe is drawn; if CD is then cancelled the wording is still true, fine.
1094. **#1322 @ 255338cd MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 1 MEDIUM, 1 LOW** (BUG-659; switch `linking.relinkKeepsRecordedDirection` ON). **(a) Traced:** the drawing-wide and scoped relinks now orient From/To by the recorded names (`NamesPutFromAtFirstEnd`), so a pipe drawn from its low end keeps P2 to P1; no record, or names that do not match the ends, keeps drawn order. **MEDIUM (wider than the PR says):** `LinkPipeEndpointsToPits` now sets `keepRecordedDirection` true for EVERY caller (CD's RegisterPipe, SetExplicitPipeOwnership, the copy clean-up, the pit relink), and that flag also switches on the Edit-Pipe-only pit-key write (`KeysFollowNames`: a name the record did not carry gets no key, a blank key CLEARS the stored one). That is probably right (it is the C-4 F1 fix), but it changes the stored pit keys the Pit Schedule trusts on every CD, COPY and relink, untested and unannounced. Either pass a separate "orient only" flag, or keep it, add a test for a reconnected pipe's keys and name it in the REGRESSION_LOG entry. **(b)** Redo-safe (no new call, same writers); no window. **LOW:** no Settings window row.
1095. **#1323 @ 2b8ab057 MERGE, 1 LOW** (BUG-677, 680). **(a) Traced:** CDEDIT batch gives each no-record pit its own next free number (claimed set seeded from every record and every batch item); CD Upsize takes the number from the record and, when the label disagrees, does not upsize and says why; CD then carries on drawing (not a hard block, R-27). **LOW:** the printed line reads "...could not be upsized: This pit's record says P5 but its label says P8. Nothing was changed. Open the pit with PITEDIT ... Continuing as drawn." Two sentences say opposite things; drop "Nothing was changed" from the rule's text on this route.
1096. **#1324 @ 4e41ad56 MERGE, 0 findings** (BUG-676). **(a) Traced:** the lid wording is remembered after the pit type and strength are set (`ApplyContextToViewModel` sets `PitType` at 1184 before line 1199), so an untouched lid keeps "Class D Heelguard Grated Lid" and unknown wording is not turned into Class C; changing strength or pit type rewrites it in the standard words, as before. 1120. **The answer first.**
   1. **BUG-698 is cured on every route that can write a size over a recorded pipe, once #1339 and #1340 are both in.** The Pit Schedule treats the size stored on the pipe as the truth and copies it into its own cell when they differ; CDSYNC was writing 100 onto the pipe, so the schedule followed. Now CDSYNC, CDUPGRADE and the drawing-recovery scan all keep the stored size. Route-by-route check in entry 1122.
   2. **Drawings already hit stay at 100.** Nothing in these PRs puts a size back. Anyone who ran CDSYNC on a drawing of converted pipes still has 100 in the schedule until they fix each pipe or undo. As far as I can tell the pipe labels still show the real size, and Edit Pipe reads the label first, so opening and saving each pipe would bring it back. That is from reading the code, not proven. Worth one carpark line and one Codex check.
   3. **#1338 test step 3 will fail as written.** CD E's pick only accepts a plain polyline (exact match). A real CE pipe object is a different object type, so AutoCAD turns it away with "That is not a polyline." before CE's new check runs. Nothing is changed, but the drafter gets the wrong reason and a Codex pass would record a FAIL. Fix in entry 1121.
1121. **#1338 @ 12ec1d1c MERGE-WITH-FIXES, 1 MEDIUM, 2 LOW** (BUG-697, CD option E). **(a) Traced:** the old check asked `PipePickService.TryResolvePipe`, whose last test is only "is it a polyline", so every plain line was refused (wrong since f93d44db, 5 Sep; never worked). It now asks `LinkService.ClassifySelectionForRibbon` (the contextual-tab classifier, one open-close read): Pipe only for a CE pipe object, a recorded pipe, CE pipe data, or a twin outline of one. A plain line returns None and goes on to the existing record, Xref, locked-layer and BUG-684 closed/curved handling. Cured. **MEDIUM (entry 1120.3):** `TryAdoptExistingLine` still calls `AddAllowedClass(typeof(Polyline), true)`. `PipePickService.cs` lines 22-30 already record that exact match refuses the CE pipe object and switched to false there. So step (3) of the REGRESSION_LOG checklist ("pick a real CE pipe: That line is already a CE pipe") cannot happen on a converted pipe. Fix: pass false here too, so the pipe object reaches the new check and gets the right words (the new check then refuses it, nothing else changes). Keep the checklist as written. **LOW 1:** the classifier skips a CE attachment (a pipe's arrow head is a polyline, `ArrowService.cs:437`) and so answers None for it. CD E would accept a pipe's own arrow as a route and, for an open one, erase it as the source, leaving the old pipe without its arrow. Rare. Refuse an attachment of a pipe or pit with "That line belongs to a CE pipe or pit. Nothing was changed." **LOW 2:** only a source guard covers the wiring (red for the right reason: the old call was present); the classifier itself is covered by the existing CivilSelection tests. **(b) Rules:** no new value, no switch (the old behaviour is the bug), open-close read only (redo-safe), no layer unlocked, Xref check kept below. Net48 and net10 compile (entry 1125). **(c) I2:** no window, no new prompt.
1122. **#1339 @ b2c0a5bb MERGE, 2 LOW** (BUG-698, CDSYNC). **(a) Traced:** `LinkCommands.TryGetPipeInfo` took the drawn width (a pipe object has none), then 100, and CDSYNC handed that to `RegisterPipe`, which writes it into the pipe's stored record AND into the pipe object (`TryPersist diameterMm`). The Pit Schedule (`PitScheduleBuilder.GetPipeDiameterDisplay`, line 1026) reads that stored record first and rewrites its own cell when they differ, so the 30 cells went to 100. The new `RecordedPipeSizeRule.Pick`: stored size, then drawn width, then 100 (last, as before). It is read by an open-close read (`ReadRecordedPipeDiameter`, redo-safe). The same reader feeds CDUPGRADE's scan (merge and replace) and CDIMPORTAUDIT's count (read only), so those are covered too. **Every path that reads a pipe size and could fall back to 100, as asked:**
   1. **Open-time upgrade to the pipe object:** refuses a pipe with no stored size ("its width is not set", `LinkService.PipeObjectUpgrade.cs:195`); never writes 100. This also means every converted pipe has a stored size, so the new rule always finds one for them. Safe.
   2. **Relink-all (RelinkAllPipesToPits, CDREPAIR):** writes no size onto a registered pipe. The "add a missing schedule row" step reads the stored size first and uses 100 only when there is no record and no width, and only for a row that does not exist. CDLINKDANGLINGPIPES registers only pipes with no record. Safe.
   3. **Edit Pipe:** the window's size comes from the label, old pipe data, the stored record, then the schedule; the drawn width is last and there is no 100. It saves what the box shows. Safe.
   4. **Drawing-recovery scan (empty schedule at open, or a pipe missing from it):** had the same 100 and `RegisterPipe` writes it back. Fixed in #1340.
   5. **Pit Schedule refresh:** reads the stored record, falls back to its own cell, never 100. Safe once the record is right.
   6. **Others found, none writes over a recorded pipe:** CDMA places its arrow as if a 100 pipe when there is no width (placement only); a copied pipe's label is offset for 100 when the copy has no record (placement only); a split builds a new schedule row with at least 100 only when no row exists, and the refresh then copies the real size in; the level propagation uses its default size only for a pipe with no record.
   **LOW 1 (not checked):** an older pipe that still carries CE's old pipe data takes THAT size first in CDSYNC (branch unchanged). Edit Pipe keeps it up to date; I did not check whether a size typed in the Pit Schedule does. If not, CDSYNC would write the older size back. Put "change a size in the Pit Schedule on an older pipe, then CDSYNC" in the Codex recipe. **LOW 2 (entry 1120.2):** no repair for drawings already hit. **(b)** No new value (100 was already hard-coded; R-00 standing); no switch: stored size first matches the Pit Schedule's own order, so the only change on Tim's PC is the bug going away. **Tests:** pure rule cases plus a source guard (red for the right reason: the old 100 line and no call). They catch a revert of the wiring, not a drawing-level regression. The Codex step is the real proof.
1123. **#1340 @ 225f0fc0 MERGE, 1 LOW** (BUG-698 second route, stacked on #1339). **(a) Traced:** `ProjectDataService.Recovery.TryGetRecoveredPipeInfo` is used by both recovery routes (`ProjectDataService.cs:761`, empty schedule: rows rebuilt from the scan; `:850`, a pipe missing from the schedule). Both then call `RegisterPipe` with the scanned size. The layer branch now asks the stored size first. The old-pipe-data branch now falls back to the stored size before 100. Cured. Two source guards, red for the right reason. **LOW:** `Pick(data.Diameter, Pick(stored, 0.0))` gives the right answer but reads oddly. `Pick(data.Diameter > 0 ? data.Diameter : stored, 0.0)` says the same thing plainly. Not needed for merge. **Merge note:** its base is the #1339 branch, so merge #1339 first, then retarget #1340 to `claude/rel-20261018b` (or merge its head, which contains #1339's commit unchanged).

With the switch OFF, pits, pipes, named sources and their level text ALREADY open Edit (3.0.51 behaviour, `DoubleClickSettings.cs` remarks); ON only adds the newer kinds and pit labels. A drafter who sees it OFF will think double-click does nothing on a pit, and one who turns it off to get AutoCAD's own double-click will not get it. **Fix:** label "Double-click also opens Edit for pit labels and the newer CE objects" (both XAML files, the catalog entry and the AutomationProperties name; the test compares label and tick text, so change all four together).

Rows below the fold would be unreachable. **Fix:** wrap the Manager Newer tools Grid in the same `ScrollViewer VerticalScrollBarVisibility="Auto" HorizontalScrollBarVisibility="Disabled"` as its sibling tab (Manager Settings is not a high-use window and its sibling page already scrolls; CDS keeps no scroll), or add a render test at 900 x 620 at 150% that proves every row is visible.

My probe on the merged trial: `TryParse("TW 12.450")` returns true, 12.45. **MEDIUM (BUG-713 only half):** the reader now skips a blank nearest spot, but an unreadable one ("abc", "P18600", or for the design-only pit offer any spot without a P) still wins the distance race and hides a good spot beside it. Rank only candidates whose text passes `SpotLevelText.TryParse` (and the P test when designOnly). **LOW:** the 9000 m limit is hard-coded (R-00 standing; the survey range in #1352 is 3000 in Settings, so two "plausible level" rules now differ); the tests were written with the fix (new file, nothing to see red). **(b)** No new Settings value, no switch (old behaviour is the bug), CDSL never blocks (Esc cancels, Enter blank), open-close reads only. **(c) I2:** no window.

Not verified in AutoCAD. Key on the drawing's full path plus the fingerprint (the BUG-271 note in `DrawingAreaUnits` already keys per drawing on the document name). **MEDIUM 2:** BUG-743 also asks for an explicit Unknown keyword: once a level is typed in a drawing, Enter can never mean "not known" there again, so a second grate whose level is unknown gets the first grate's level.
